\displaystyle\left|\lambda_{1}-\frac{2}{e^{4m}-1}\right|\leq 2e^{4m}\frac{\left|1-e^{-\Lambda_{1}}\right|}{\left|e^{4m-\Lambda_{1}}-1\right|\left(e^{4m}-1\right)}.

One can estimate each of the terms of the last factor. Notice that numerator can be written as

\displaystyle|1-e^{-\Lambda_{1}}|=\left|\int_{-1}^{0}\left(\frac{d}{ds}e^{s\Lambda_{1}}\right)\mbox{d}s\right|=\left|\Lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{1}e^{-s\Lambda_{1}}\mbox{d}s\right|\leq|\Lambda_{1}|e^{|\Lambda_{1}|}.

In addition

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{1}|\leq 2\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{|\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})+\tfrac{1}{2}|}{1+\lambda_{1}-\bar{z}}\mbox{d}\bar{z}\leq\frac{C\kappa}{\lambda_{1}}\leq\frac{C\kappa}{\lambda^{*}(M)},

where
C
is a universal constant coming from Lemma
2.2
. For the denominator we just note that

\displaystyle e^{4m-\Lambda_{1}}-1\geq e^{4-\frac{C\kappa}{\lambda^{*}(M)}}-1\geq e^{2}-1>1\qquad\text{if}\quad\tfrac{C\kappa}{\lambda^{*}(M)}<2.

Then, taking
\kappa<\kappa_{0}\equiv\min(\kappa^{\prime}_{0},\kappa^{\prime\prime}_{0})
, where
\kappa^{\prime}_{0}
is given by Lemma
5.9
and
\kappa^{\prime\prime}_{0}=\frac{2\lambda^{*}(M)}{C}
, we find that

\displaystyle\left|\lambda_{1}-\frac{2}{e^{4m}-1}\right|\leq C(M)\kappa.

for all
0\leq\kappa<\kappa_{0}
and
1\leq m<M
.
∎

To sum up, Proposition
5.7
shows that

\displaystyle\|A_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle C(M),

\displaystyle\|A_{1}\|_{L^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle C(M)\left(\|a_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|+|\lambda^{\varepsilon}_{2}|\|a_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\|B_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle C(M),

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{L^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle C(M)\left(\|b_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\|b_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|+\|a_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\right).

In the following two lemmas we are going to learn how to invert the left hand side of (
59
).

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
\lambda_{1}
be as in Lemma
5.9
and let
F\in L^{2}([-1,1])
satisfying
(F,\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}b_{0})_{L^{2}}=0.
Then

f(z):=F(z)b_{0}(z)=\frac{F(z)}{1+\lambda_{1}-z},

solves

(1+\lambda_{1}-z)f(z)+\frac{1}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})f(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}=F(z).

Proof.

Direct computation.
∎

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
\lambda_{1}
be as in Lemma
5.9
and let
G\in L^{2}([-1,1])
. Then, if

\displaystyle\lambda:=\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}G(\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}}{\int_{-1}^{1}b_{0}(\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}},

the function

\displaystyle f(z):=(G(z)-\lambda b_{0}(z))b_{0}(z)=\frac{G(z)-\lambda b_{0}(z)}{1+\lambda_{1}-z},

solves

(66)

\displaystyle(1+\lambda_{1}-z)f(z)+\frac{1}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})f(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}+\lambda b_{0}(z)=G(z).

In addition,

(67)

\displaystyle|\lambda|\leq C(M)\|G\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}.

Proof.

It is easy to check that
F(z):=G(z)-\lambda b_{0}
satisfies
(F,\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}b_{0})_{L^{2}}=0
by the definition of
\lambda
. Then, we can apply directly Lemma
5.10
to obtain (
66
). To conclude (
67
), we note that

\left|\int_{-1}^{1}G(\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}\right|\leq\|G\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}\|b_{0}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}\|\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}\leq C(M)\|G\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])},

and since
b_{0}(z)>(2+\lambda_{1})^{-1}
we get

\displaystyle\left|\int_{-1}^{1}b_{0}(\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}z\right|\geq\frac{1}{2+\lambda_{1}}\int_{-1}^{1}-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z)b_{0}(z)dz=\frac{2m}{2+\lambda_{1}},

where in the last step we have used (
63
). Combining both we have

|\lambda|\leq\frac{2+\lambda_{1}}{2m}C(M)\|G\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}\leq C(M)\|G\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}.

Here we have used that
\lambda_{1}<C
where
C
is a universal constant. This is easy to check from

\displaystyle I(\mu)=\frac{1}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})}{1+\mu-\bar{z}}\mbox{d}\bar{z}\leq C\log\left(1+\frac{2}{\mu}\right),

since for
\mu
big enough we get that
I(\mu)<1
. Recall that
\lambda_{1}
solves the equation
I(\mu)=1
.
∎

Lemma
5.11
implies that if we solve

(68)

\displaystyle 2a^{\varepsilon}_{2}(z)+\frac{e^{-2m}}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})b^{\varepsilon}_{1}(\bar{z})e^{-m\varepsilon(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}=A_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}](z)+\varepsilon A_{1}[a^{\varepsilon}_{2},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};a_{1},b_{0},\lambda_{1}](z),

(69)

\displaystyle b_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z)=\left(B_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}](z)+\varepsilon B_{1}[a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)-\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}b_{0}(z)\right)b_{0}(z),

(70)

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}=\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}(B_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}](\bar{z})+\varepsilon B_{1}[a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](\bar{z}))b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}}{\int_{-1}^{1}b_{0}(\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}},

we find a solution of (
58
) and (
59
). Now, to obtain a solution of (
68
), (
69
) and (
70
) we firstly introduce (
69
) and (
70
) in the second term of the left hand side of (
68
) and (
70
) in the right hand side of (
69
). In this way, a solution of

(71)

\displaystyle 2a^{\varepsilon}_{2}(z)=\tilde{A}_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}](z)+\varepsilon\tilde{A}_{1}[a^{\varepsilon}_{2},b^{\varepsilon}_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};a_{1},b_{0},\lambda_{1}](z),

(72)

\displaystyle b_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z)=\tilde{B}_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}](z)+\varepsilon\tilde{B}_{1}[a^{\varepsilon}_{2},b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z),

(73)

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}=\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}(B_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}](\bar{z})+\varepsilon B_{1}[a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](\bar{z}))b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}}{\int_{-1}^{1}b_{0}(\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}},

where

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{0}[a_{1},\,b_{0}](z)

\displaystyle=A_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}](z)-\frac{e^{-2m}}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})B_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}](\bar{z})e^{-m\varepsilon(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}

\displaystyle+\frac{e^{-2m}}{2m}\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}B_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}](\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}}{\int_{-1}^{1}b_{0}(\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}}\left(\int_{-1}^{1}b_{0}(\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})e^{-m\varepsilon(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}\right),

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{1}[a_{2}^{\varepsilon},\,b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};a_{1},b_{0}](z)

\displaystyle=A_{1}[a_{2}^{\varepsilon},\,b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};a_{1},b_{0}](z)-\frac{e^{-2m}}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{z}(\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})B_{1}[a_{2}^{\varepsilon},\,b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon m(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}

\displaystyle+\frac{e^{-2m}}{2m}\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}B_{1}[a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}}{\int_{-1}^{1}b_{0}(\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}}\left(\int_{-1}^{1}b_{0}(\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})e^{-m\varepsilon(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}\right),

and

\displaystyle\tilde{B}_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}](z)

\displaystyle=b_{0}(z)\left(B_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}](z)-b_{0}(z)\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}B_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}](\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}}{\int_{-1}^{1}b_{0}(\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}}\right)

\displaystyle\tilde{B}_{1}[a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)

\displaystyle=b_{0}(z)\left(B_{1}[a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)-b_{0}(z)\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}B_{1}[a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}}{\int_{-1}^{1}b_{0}(\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}}\right),

will give us a solution of (
68
), (
69
) and (
70
).

Finally, in order to solve (
71
), (
72
) and (
73
) we can use a contraction argument on parameter
\varepsilon
. Indeed, let us call
F_{A}[a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};a_{1},b_{0}](z)
,
F_{B}[a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};a_{1},b_{0}](z)
,
F_{\lambda}[a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};a_{1},b_{0}](z)
to the right hand side of (
71
), (
72
) and (
73
) respectively. We define the constants values

C_{A}:=\|\tilde{A}_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}]\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])},\quad C_{B}:=\|\tilde{B}_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}]\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])},

and

\displaystyle C_{\lambda}:=\left|\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}B_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}](\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}}{\int_{-1}^{1}b_{0}(\bar{z})b_{0}(\bar{z})\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}}\right|\leq C(M).

Note that the system (
71
), (
72
) and (
73
) is quadratic because of the products
\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}a_{2}^{\varepsilon}
and
\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}b_{1}^{\varepsilon}
. Thus, we first check that, if
\|2a_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<2C_{A}
,
\|b_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<2C_{B}
and
|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|<2C_{\lambda}
for
\varepsilon
small enough (with respect to a constant that just depends on
M
), then

\displaystyle\|F_{A}[a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};a_{1},b_{0}]\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<2C_{A},

\displaystyle\|F_{B}[a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};a_{1},b_{0}]\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<2C_{B},

\displaystyle|F_{\lambda}[a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};a_{1},b_{0}]|<2C_{\lambda}.

In addition, for any pair
u=(2a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon})
and
\tilde{u}=(2\tilde{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{\lambda}_{2}^{\varepsilon})
satisfying
\|2a_{2}^{\varepsilon},2\tilde{a}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<2C_{A}
,
\|b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<2C_{B}
and
|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}_{2}|<2C_{\lambda}
we have that

\displaystyle\|F_{A}[u;a_{1},b_{0}]-F_{A}[\tilde{u};a_{1},b_{0}]\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}\leq\varepsilon C(M)\|u-\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])\times\mathbb{R}},

\displaystyle\|F_{B}[u;a_{1},b_{0}]-F_{B}[\tilde{u};a_{1},b_{0}]\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}\leq\varepsilon C(M)\|u-\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])\times\mathbb{R}},

\displaystyle|F_{\lambda}[u;a_{1},b_{0}]-F_{\lambda}[\tilde{u};a_{1},b_{0}]|\leq\varepsilon C(M)\|u-\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])\times\mathbb{R}}.

Taking
\varepsilon_{0}:=C(M)^{-1},
we have that right hand side of (
68
), (
69
), (
70
) is a contraction mapping for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}(M).
This implies that there exist
(a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon})\in L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])\times\mathbb{R}
, with
\|2a_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<2C_{A}
,
\|b_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<{2C_{B}}
and
|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|<2C_{\lambda}
solving (
71
), (
72
) and (
73
), for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}(M)
.

Remark 5.12
.

Let us emphasize that
C_{A}
,
C_{B}
and
C_{\lambda}
are uniformly bounded by some
C(M).

Therefore, we have shown the existence of a solution
(\lambda,a,b)\in\mathbb{R}\times L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])
solving (
5.3.1
) and (
5.3.1
), with

(74)

\displaystyle a(z)=

\displaystyle\,a_{1}\varepsilon+a_{2}^{\varepsilon}(z)\varepsilon^{2},

(75)

\displaystyle b(z)=

\displaystyle\,b_{0}(z)+b_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z)\varepsilon,

(76)

\displaystyle\lambda=

\displaystyle\,1+\lambda_{1}\varepsilon+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{2},

and

(77)

\displaystyle\|a_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}\leq C(M),\quad\|b_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}\leq C(M),\quad|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|\leq C(M).

Next we shall show that this solution is smooth.

5.3.2.
Regularity

In order to determine the precise regularity of the constructed solution
(a,b)
we proceed as follows. In first place, we recall that the system (
5.3.1
), (
5.3.1
) can be written as

(78)

\displaystyle(\lambda+1-\varepsilon+\varepsilon z-\varepsilon^{2}\Phi_{\kappa}(z))a(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})\left(a(\bar{z})e^{-m\varepsilon|z-\bar{z}|}-b(\bar{z})e^{-m|2+\varepsilon(z+\bar{z})|}\right)\mbox{d}\bar{z},

(79)

\displaystyle(\lambda-1+\varepsilon-\varepsilon z+\varepsilon^{2}\Phi_{\kappa}(z))b(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})\left(a(\bar{z})e^{-m|2+\varepsilon(z+\bar{z})|}-b(\bar{z})e^{-m\varepsilon|z-\bar{z}|}\right)\mbox{d}\bar{z}.

Notice that neither
\lambda+1-\varepsilon+\varepsilon z-\varepsilon^{2}\Phi_{\kappa}(z)
nor
\lambda-1+\varepsilon-\varepsilon z+\varepsilon^{2}\Phi_{\kappa}(z)
have zeros on the unit interval. More specifically, for the computed eigenvalue
\lambda=1+\lambda_{1}\varepsilon+O(\varepsilon^{2})
we have that

\displaystyle\lambda+1-\varepsilon+\varepsilon z-\varepsilon^{2}\Phi_{\kappa}(z)

\displaystyle=2+O(\varepsilon)\geq 1,

\displaystyle\lambda-1+\varepsilon-\varepsilon z+\varepsilon^{2}\Phi_{\kappa}(z)

\displaystyle=(1+\lambda_{1}-z)\varepsilon+O(\varepsilon^{2})\geq\varepsilon(\lambda_{1}+O(\varepsilon)).

As we have proved before that
\lambda_{1}
is strictly positive, see Lemma
5.9
, taking
\varepsilon
small enough these two quantities are not zero and we are allow to divide both sides of (
78
), (
79
) by these factors:

\displaystyle a(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon}{2m(\lambda+1-\varepsilon+\varepsilon z-\varepsilon^{2}\Phi_{\kappa}(z))}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})\left(a(\bar{z})e^{-m\varepsilon|z-\bar{z}|}-b(\bar{z})e^{-m(2+\varepsilon(z+\bar{z}))}\right)\mbox{d}\bar{z},

\displaystyle b(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon}{2m(\lambda-1+\varepsilon-\varepsilon z+\varepsilon^{2}\Phi_{\kappa}(z))}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})\left(a(\bar{z})e^{-m(2+\varepsilon(z+\bar{z}))}-b(\bar{z})e^{-m\varepsilon|z-\bar{z}|}\right)\mbox{d}\bar{z}.

Since these terms are the factors that appear multiplying the solution
(a,b)
in the left hand side of (
78
), (
79
) we have that their inverses are
C^{\infty}-
functions on the unit interval
[-1,1]
. In addition, the integral terms

\displaystyle e^{-m\varepsilon z}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})a(\bar{z})e^{-m\varepsilon\bar{z}}\mbox{d}\bar{z}\quad\text{and}\quad e^{-m\varepsilon z}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})b(\bar{z})e^{-m\varepsilon\bar{z}}\mbox{d}\bar{z},

are also
C^{\infty}-
functions. Finally, the remaining integral term
f(z)=\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})a(\bar{z})e^{-m\varepsilon|z-\bar{z}|}\mbox{d}\bar{z}
is in
H^{2}([-1,1])
for
a\in L^{2}([-1,1])
. To see this one just has to take two derivatives on
f(z)
to get that

\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime}(z)=-2m\varepsilon\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z)a(z)+(m\varepsilon)^{2}f(z).

In fact, we have that
f(z)\in H^{k+2}([-1,1])
if
a\in H^{k}([-1,1])
. The same hods for

\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}(\bar{z})b(\bar{z})e^{-m\varepsilon|z-\bar{z}|}\mbox{d}\bar{z}.

All this yields that if
(a,b)\in H^{k}([-1,1])\times H^{k}([-1,1])
then
(a,b)\in H^{k+2}([-1,1])\times H^{k+2}([-1,1])
for all
k\geq 0.
Then we have proved that
(a,b)\in C^{\infty}([-1,1])
.

5.3.3.
Proof of uniqueness

Until now we have shown that there exist
\lambda
and
(w_{n}^{+},w_{n}^{-})
given by

\displaystyle w^{+}_{n}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}0&n\neq m,\\
a(z)&n=m,\end{array}\right.\quad\text{and}\quad w^{-}_{n}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}0&n\neq m,\\
b(z)&n=m,\end{array}\right.

with
(\lambda,a,b)\in\mathbb{R}\times C^{\infty}([-1,1])\times C^{\infty}([-1,1])
solving (
5.2
), (
5.2
) for all
n\geq 1
and satisfying (
74
), (
75
), (
76
) and (
77
).

To finish the proof of Theorem
5.1
we need to check that the kernel of
\mathcal{L}[\lambda]
, fixed that
\lambda
, is one dimensional. Therefore we have to prove that the solution of system (
5.2
), (
5.2
), given by (
5.3.1
), (
5.3.1
), (
74
), (
75
), for
\lambda
given by (
76
), is unique modulo multiplication by a constant.
In order to prove our goal, we will distinguish between two cases:

•

Case
n\neq m:
Let us postpone this case to next section
5.4.2
(take
W^{\pm}_{n}=0
on that section).

•

Case
n=m:
Let
(u,v)\in L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])
be a solution of (
5.3.1
), (
5.3.1
), with
\lambda
given by (
76
). Note that
(u,v)
depends on
\varepsilon
although we do not make explicit this dependence. Then, let us see that for some constant
C
we have

\begin{cases}u(z)=Ca(z),\\
v(z)=Cb(z).\end{cases}

The system (
5.3.1
), (
5.3.1
) is linear in
(u,v)
and we can assume without loss of generality that

\|u\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}^{2}+\|v\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}^{2}=1.

If it is not the case we only need to normalized the solution and enter that value into the final constant
C
. Now, using (
5.3.1
), we get that
u(z)=\varepsilon u_{1}(z)
with
\|u_{1}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}=O(1)
in terms of
\varepsilon
. This information yields from (
5.3.1
) that

(80)

\displaystyle(1+\lambda_{1}-z)v(z)+\frac{1}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})v(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}=F(z),

where
\|F\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}=O(\varepsilon)
. Just dividing (
80
) by
(1+\lambda_{1}-z)
we have

(81)

\displaystyle v(z)=\frac{C}{1+\lambda_{1}-z}+\frac{F(z)}{1+\lambda_{1}-z}.

In addition, recalling the precise form of
b_{0}(z)
we obtain that

(82)

\displaystyle v(z)=Cb_{0}(z)+\varepsilon v_{1}(z),

where
\|v_{1}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}=O(1)
in terms of
\varepsilon
and
C
is a constant.

Looking again (
5.3.1
) we have that

\displaystyle 2u_{1}(z)=C\frac{e^{-2m}}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z}))b_{0}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}+G(z),

where
\|G\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}=O(\varepsilon)
. Recalling the precise form of
a_{1}
, see (
57
), forces to

(83)

\displaystyle u_{1}(z)=Ca_{1}+\varepsilon u_{2}(z),

with
\|u_{2}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}=O(1)
in terms of
\varepsilon
and the same constant
C
than in (
82
).

Finally, we get a coupled system for
(u_{2},v_{1})
which is exactly the same as (
68
), (
69
) for
(a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon})
up to the multiplicative constant
C
. Indeed, we have, from (
82
) together with (
60
) and (
83
) together with (
57
), that

\displaystyle 2u_{2}(z)+\frac{e^{-2m}}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}(\bar{z})v_{1}(\bar{z})e^{-m\varepsilon(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}=CA_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}](z)+\varepsilon A_{1}[u_{2};\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon},Ca_{1},Cb_{0}](z),

\displaystyle v_{1}(z)=b_{0}(z)(CB_{0}[a_{1},b_{0}](z)+\varepsilon B_{1}[u_{2},v_{1};\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)-C\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}b_{0}(z)).

This system is linear in
(u_{2},v_{1})
(now
\lambda
is fixed). One can check that
C(a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon})
is a solution and by a fixed point argument must be unique. Then
(u_{2},v_{1})=C(a_{2}^{\varepsilon},b_{1}^{\varepsilon})
(with the same constant
C
as in (
83
) and (
82
)) and consequently we have proved our goal.

5.4.
Codimension of the image of the linear operator

Let
\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
given by Theorem
5.1
. In order to determine the codimension in
Y
of the linear operator
\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\equiv D_{\mathsf{f}}F[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m},0]
on
X
, from expression (
36
) we see that we have to study the equation:

(84)

\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]h=H,

with
h\in X
and
H\in Y.
As we did before, we will use the expansions

h({\bf x})=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}h_{n}(y)\cos(nx),\qquad H({\bf x})=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}H_{n}(y)\sin(nx),

and, similarly to what we made in section
5.1
, we define the auxiliary functions

\displaystyle H_{n}^{\pm}(y)

\displaystyle:=H_{n}(\pm y)\qquad\text{for}\quad y\in[1-\varepsilon,1+\varepsilon],

\displaystyle h_{n}^{\pm}(y)

\displaystyle:=h_{n}(\pm y)\qquad\,\,\text{for}\quad y\in[1-\varepsilon,1+\varepsilon],

and

\displaystyle W_{n}^{\pm}(z)

\displaystyle:=H_{n}^{\pm}(1+\varepsilon z)\qquad\text{for}\quad z\in[-1,1],

\displaystyle w_{n}^{\pm}(z)

\displaystyle:=h_{n}^{\pm}(1+\varepsilon z)\qquad\,\,\text{for}\quad z\in[-1,1].

Then, for any
\varepsilon>0
, the functional equation (
84
) is equivalent to solve the system

(85)

-n(\lambda_{m}+1-\varepsilon+\varepsilon z-\varepsilon^{2}\Phi_{\kappa}(z))w_{n}^{+}(z)\\
+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})w_{n}^{+}(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon n|z-\bar{z}|}\mbox{d}\bar{z}-\frac{\varepsilon e^{-2n}}{2}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})w_{n}^{-}(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon n(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}=W^{+}_{n}(z),

(86)

-n(\lambda_{m}-1+\varepsilon-\varepsilon z+\varepsilon^{2}\Phi_{\kappa}(z))w_{n}^{-}(z)\\
+\frac{\varepsilon e^{-2n}}{2}\int_{1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})w_{n}^{+}(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon n(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})w_{n}^{-}(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon n|z-\bar{z}|}\mbox{d}\bar{z}=W^{-}_{n}(z),

for all
n\geq 1.
Here
\lambda_{m}\equiv\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
. In what follow we will omit the dependence on both
\varepsilon
and
\kappa
of
\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
and keep that one on
m
.
Let us define the function

\displaystyle\Lambda^{+}_{m}(z):=

\displaystyle\lambda_{m}+1+\varepsilon(-1+z)-\varepsilon^{2}\Phi_{\kappa}(z),

\displaystyle\Lambda^{-}_{m}(z):=

\displaystyle\lambda_{m}-1+\varepsilon(+1-z)+\varepsilon^{2}\Phi_{\kappa}(z),

and the operators

(87)

\displaystyle T_{n}^{+}[u,v](z):=

\displaystyle\Lambda_{m}^{+}(z)u(z)-\frac{\varepsilon}{2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})u(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon n|z-\bar{z}|}\mbox{d}\bar{z}+\frac{\varepsilon e^{-2n}}{2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})v(\bar{z})e^{-n\varepsilon(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z},

(88)

\displaystyle T_{n}^{-}[u,v](z):=

\displaystyle\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)v(z)+\frac{\varepsilon}{2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})v(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon n|z-\bar{z}|}\mbox{d}\bar{z}-\frac{\varepsilon e^{-2n}}{2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})u(\bar{z})e^{-n\varepsilon(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}.

Note that
T_{n}^{+}
and
T_{n}^{-}
are nothing but
\tfrac{1}{n}L^{+}_{n}[\lambda_{m}]
and
\tfrac{1}{n}L^{-}_{n}[\lambda_{m}]
respectively, with
L^{+}_{n}
and
L^{-}_{n}
given by (
5.2
) and (
5.2
). Therefore, the system (
85
), (
86
) is equivalent to

(89)

\displaystyle T_{n}^{+}[w^{+}_{n},w^{-}_{n}](z)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}W_{n}^{+}(z),

(90)

\displaystyle T_{n}^{-}[w^{+}_{n},w^{-}_{n}](z)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}W_{n}^{-}(z),

for all
n\geq 1.

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
f,g,u,v\in L^{2}([-1,1])
then

\displaystyle-(T_{n}^{+}[u,v],\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}f)_{L^{2}}+(T^{-}_{n}[u,v],\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}g)_{L^{2}}=-(\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}u,T^{+}_{n}[f,g])_{L^{2}}+(\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}v,T^{-}_{n}[f,g])_{L^{2}}.

Proof.

Direct computation.
∎

Lemma
5.13
implies the following necessary condition for (
89
), (
90
) in order to be solvable:

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
(a,b)\in L^{2}([-1,1])
given by (
74
) and (
75
). That is, the pair
(a,b)
solves

\displaystyle T^{+}_{m}[a,b]=0,\qquad T^{-}_{m}[a,b]=0.

Then

\displaystyle-(W^{+}_{m},\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}a)_{L^{2}}+(W^{-}_{m},\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}b)_{L^{2}}=0

Proof.

Direct computation.
∎

Next we state and prove the main theorem of this section:

Theorem 5.15
.

Let
M>1
. There exist
\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(M)
and
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(M)
such that for all
0\leq\kappa<\kappa_{0}
,
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
and
1\leq m<M
, the following statement hold:

Let
(\lambda_{m},a,b)\in\mathbb{R}\times L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])
given by (
74
), (
75
) and (
76
) and
\{W_{n}^{\pm}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sum_{k=0}^{3-j}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n^{2k}\|\partial^{j}_{z}W_{n}^{\pm}\|^{2}_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<\infty.

and

\displaystyle-(W^{+}_{m},\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}a)_{L^{2}}+(W^{-}_{m},\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}b)_{L^{2}}=0.

Then, there exist
\{w^{\pm}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
, with

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{3}\sum_{k=0}^{4-j}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n^{2k}\|\partial^{j}_{z}w_{n}^{\pm}\|^{2}_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<\infty,

satisfying (
89
) and (
90
) for all
n\geq 1.

Proof.

The proof will be split into two cases. The case
n=m
and the case
n\neq m
.

5.4.1.
The case
n=m
.

To deal with this case we introduce the space
L^{2}([-1,1])
with weight
-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}.
That is,
L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}([-1,1])
with inner-product and norm given by

(u,v)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}:=\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z}))u(\bar{z})v(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z},

and

\|v\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}:=(v,v)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}=\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z}))|v(\bar{z})|^{2}\mbox{d}\bar{z}.

In addition, we define the bilinear forms

\displaystyle B[(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})]

\displaystyle:=(T^{+}_{m}[u_{1},v_{1}],u_{2})_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+(T^{-}_{m}[u_{1},v_{1}],v_{2})_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}[v_{1},v_{2}]

\displaystyle:=\int_{-1}^{1}(1+\lambda_{1}-\bar{z})v_{1}(\bar{z})v_{2}(\bar{z})(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z}))\mbox{d}\bar{z}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2m}\left(\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z}))v_{1}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}\right)\left(\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z}))v_{2}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}\right).

We start with the properties of the bilinear form
\mathcal{B}
. Notice that by definition (
60
) of
b_{0}
we have

\mathcal{B}[b_{0},b_{0}]=0.

We also define the orthogonal complement of
b_{0}
in
L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}([-1,1]):

b_{0}^{\perp}:=\{v\in L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}([-1,1])\mid(b_{0},v)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}=0\}.

Lemma 5.16
.

Let
v_{1},v_{2}\in L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}([-1,1])
. Then, the following estimate holds

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}[v_{1},v_{2}]|

\displaystyle\leq C\|v_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}\|v_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}},

where the constant
C
is independent of
\varepsilon,\kappa,m.
Moreover, if
v\in b_{0}^{\perp}
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}[v,v]\geq c(M)||v||_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}.

Proof.

The first conclusion is trivial. For the other one we need to proceed as follow. In first place, we fix an arbitrary
v\in b_{0}^{\perp}
, i.e.
v\in L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}([-1,1])
such that

\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z)v(z)b_{0}(z)\,\mbox{d}z=0.

Notice that by definition

(91)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}[v,v]=\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z))\frac{|v(z)|^{2}}{b_{0}(z)}dz-\frac{1}{2m}\left(\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))v(z)\,\mbox{d}z\right)^{2}.

In addition, for all
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
, we have that

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))v(z)\,\mbox{d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))(1-\mu b_{0}(z))v(z)\,\mbox{d}z=\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))}{\sqrt{b_{0}(z)}}\sqrt{b_{0}(z)}\left(1-\mu b_{0}(z)\right)v(z)\,\mbox{d}z

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))\frac{|v(z)|^{2}}{b_{0}(z)}\,\mbox{d}z\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))b_{0}(z)(1-\mu b_{0}(z))^{2}\,\mbox{d}z\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Thus

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m}\left(\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))v(z)\,\mbox{d}z\right)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2m}\left(\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))b_{0}(z)(1-\mu b_{0}(z))^{2}\,\mbox{d}z\right)\left(\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))\frac{|v(z)|^{2}}{b_{0}(z)}\,\mbox{d}z\right)

and consequently

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}[v,v]\geq\left(1-\frac{1}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z))b_{0}(z)(1-\mu b_{0}(z))^{2}\,\mbox{d}z\right)\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z))(1+\lambda_{1}-z)|v(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z.

Expanding the square we have

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z))b_{0}(z)(1-\mu b_{0}(z))^{2}\,\mbox{d}z=2m-2\mu\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))|b_{0}(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z+\mu^{2}\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z))(b_{0}(z))^{3}\,\mbox{d}z,

and therefore

1-\frac{1}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z))b_{0}(z)(1-\mu b_{0}(z))^{2}\,\mbox{d}z\\
=\frac{\mu}{2m}\left(2\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))|b_{0}(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z-\mu\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z))(b_{0}(z))^{3}\,\mbox{d}z\right).

Choosing

\mu=\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z))(b_{0}(z))^{2}\,\mbox{d}z}{\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z))(b_{0}(z))^{3}\,\mbox{d}z},

we obtain the lower bound

\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z))b_{0}(z)(1-\mu b_{0}(z))^{2}\,\mbox{d}z=\frac{\mu}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z))|b_{0}(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z\geq c(M).

Finally, as
\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{1}^{\ast}(M)
we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}[v,v]\geq c(M)\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))(1+\lambda_{1}-z)|v(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z\geq c(M)\lambda_{1}\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}\geq c(M)\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}.

∎

Next we shall study the bilinear for
B[(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})]
.

Lemma 5.17
.

Let
u,v\in L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}([-1,1])
. Then, the following expression holds

(92)

\displaystyle B[(u,v),(u,v)]=

\displaystyle 2\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z))|u(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z+\varepsilon\mathcal{B}[v,v]+\varepsilon B_{1}[u,u]+\varepsilon^{2}B_{2}[v,v],

with
B_{1},B_{2}
given by (
95
), (
96
) and satisfying

\displaystyle|B_{1}[u,u]|\leq C\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2},\qquad|B_{2}[v,v]|\leq C\|v\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}},

where the constant
C
is independent of
\varepsilon,\kappa,m.

Proof.

From
(
87
)
and (
88
) we find that

\displaystyle B[(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})]=

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))\Lambda^{+}_{m}(z)u_{1}(z)u_{2}(z)\,\mbox{d}z

\displaystyle+\frac{\varepsilon}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})u_{1}(z)u_{2}(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon n|z-\bar{z}|}\,\mbox{d}z\mbox{d}\bar{z}

\displaystyle-\frac{\varepsilon e^{-2m}}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})v_{1}(z)u_{2}(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon m(z+\bar{z})}\,\mbox{d}z\mbox{d}\bar{z}

\displaystyle+\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))\Lambda^{-}_{m}(z)v_{1}(z)v_{2}(z)\,\mbox{d}z

\displaystyle-\frac{\varepsilon}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})v_{1}(z)v_{2}(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon m|z-\bar{z}|}\,\mbox{d}z\mbox{d}\bar{z}

\displaystyle+\frac{\varepsilon e^{-2m}}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})u_{1}(z)v_{2}(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon m(z+\bar{z})}\,\mbox{d}z\mbox{d}\bar{z}.

From the above expression we can check that

(93)

\displaystyle|B[(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})]|\leq C\left(\|u_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+\|v_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}\right)\left(\|u_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+\|v_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}\right),

where the constant
C
is independent of
\varepsilon,\kappa,m.
In addition, if
(u_{1},v_{1})=(u,v)=(u_{2},v_{2})
the cross terms cancels and we obtain

\displaystyle B[(u,v),(u,v)]=

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))\Lambda^{+}_{m}(z)|u(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z

\displaystyle+\frac{\varepsilon}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})u(z)u(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon m|z-\bar{z}|}\,\mbox{d}z\mbox{d}\bar{z}

\displaystyle+\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))\Lambda^{-}_{m}(z)|v(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z

\displaystyle-\frac{\varepsilon}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})v(z)v(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon m|z-\bar{z}|}\,\mbox{d}z\mbox{d}\bar{z}

\displaystyle:=I+II.

where

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))\Lambda^{+}_{m}(z)|u(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z+\frac{\varepsilon}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})u(z)u(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon m|z-\bar{z}|}\,\mbox{d}z\mbox{d}\bar{z},

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle=\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))\Lambda^{-}_{m}(z)|v(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z-\frac{\varepsilon}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})v(z)v(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon m|z-\bar{z}|}\,\mbox{d}z\mbox{d}\bar{z}.

We recall that

\displaystyle\Lambda^{+}_{m}(z)

\displaystyle=2+(-1+\lambda_{1}+z)\varepsilon+(\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}-\Phi_{\kappa}(z))\varepsilon^{2},

(94)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{-}_{m}(z)

\displaystyle=(1+\lambda_{1}-z)\varepsilon+(\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}+\Phi_{\kappa}(z))\varepsilon^{2}.

Thus

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=2\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))|u(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z+\varepsilon\left(\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))\left[(-1+\lambda_{1}+z)+\varepsilon(\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}-\Phi_{\kappa}(z))\right]|u(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\varepsilon}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})u(z)u(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon m|z-\bar{z}|}\,\mbox{d}z\mbox{d}\bar{z},

and

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z))\frac{|v(z)|^{2}}{b_{0}(z)}\,\mbox{d}z+\varepsilon^{2}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z)(\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}+\Phi_{\kappa}(z))|v(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z\pm\frac{\varepsilon}{2m}\left(\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z)v(z)\,\mbox{d}z\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\mathcal{B}[v,v]+\varepsilon^{2}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z)(\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}+\Phi_{\kappa}(z))|v(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z)\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})v(z)v(\bar{z})\frac{e^{-\varepsilon m|z-\bar{z}|}-1}{\varepsilon}\,\mbox{d}z\mbox{d}\bar{z},

where in the last step we have used the expression (
91
).

Putting all together, we have

\displaystyle B[(u,v),(u,v)]=

\displaystyle 2\int_{-1}^{1}(-\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z))|u(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z+\varepsilon\mathcal{B}[v,v]+\varepsilon B_{1}[u,u]+\varepsilon^{2}B_{2}[v,v],

where

(95)

\displaystyle B_{1}[u,u]:=

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z)\left[(-1+\lambda_{1}+z)+\varepsilon(\lambda^{\varepsilon}_{2}-\Phi_{\kappa}(z))\right]|u(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z)\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})u(z)u(\bar{z})e^{-m\varepsilon|z-\bar{z}|}\,\mbox{d}z\mbox{d}\bar{z},

(96)

\displaystyle B_{2}[v,v]:=

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z)(\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}+\Phi_{\kappa}(z))|v(z)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}z

\displaystyle-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z)\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})v(z)v(\bar{z})\frac{e^{-\varepsilon m|z-\bar{z}|}-1}{\varepsilon}\,\mbox{d}z\mbox{d}\bar{z}.

Finally, one can see that

\displaystyle|B_{1}[u,u]|\leq C\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2},

\displaystyle|B_{2}[v,v]|\leq C\|v\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}},

for some constant
C
, which is independent of
\varepsilon,\kappa,m.

∎

Combining all the previous results, we are in a good position to prove the following lemma. Before that, we recall the solution
(a,b)
defined in previous section
5.3.1
and given by (
74
), (
75
).
We also define the orthogonal complement of
(-a,b)
in
L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}([-1,1])\times L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}([-1,1])
:

(-a,b)^{\perp}:=\left\{(u,v)\in L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}([-1,1])\times L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}([-1,1])\mid-(u,a)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+(v,b)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}=0\right\}.

Lemma 5.18
.

Let
M>1.
There exist
\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(M)
,
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(M)
such that for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
,
0\leq\kappa<\kappa_{0}
and
1\leq m<M
, the following estimates hold

\displaystyle|B[(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})]|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|u_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+\|v_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}\right)\left(\|u_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+\|v_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}\right),

(97)

\displaystyle B[(u,v),(u,v)]

\displaystyle\geq c(M)\left(\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}\right),

for all
(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{2},v_{2})\in L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}([-1,1])\times L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}([-1,1])
and for all
(u,v)\in(-a,b)^{\perp}.

Proof.

Notice that the first estimate had been proved in (
93
). To prove (
97
) we start using the fact that
(u,v)\in(-a,b)^{\perp},
which implies that

(v,b)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}=(u,a)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}.

Recalling expressions (
74
) and (
75
) we see that

\displaystyle(v,b_{0})_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}=\varepsilon(u,a_{1})_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+\varepsilon^{2}(u,a_{2}^{\varepsilon})_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}-\varepsilon(v,b_{1}^{\varepsilon})_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}.

Thus we can assume that
v=v_{0}+\varepsilon v_{1}
, where
v_{0}\in b_{0}^{\perp}
and
v_{1}\in\text{span}\{b_{0}\}
. So, we have

(v_{1},b_{0})_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}=(u,a_{1})_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+\varepsilon(u,a_{2}^{\varepsilon})_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}-(v,b_{1}^{\varepsilon})_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}.

By the explicit expressions of
b_{0}
and
a_{1}
together with the upper bounds (
77
), we obtain

(98)

\|v_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}\leq C(M)(\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}).

By the definition of
v
as the sum of two orthogonal functions we have

(99)

\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}=\|v_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|v_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}.

In addition, combining (
98
), (
99
) we get

(100)

\displaystyle\|v_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}-\varepsilon^{2}\|v_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}\geq\|v\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}-C(M)\varepsilon^{2}(\|u\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2})

\displaystyle\geq c(M)\|v\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}-C(M)\varepsilon^{2}\|u\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}},

for
\varepsilon
small enough.

We then can estimate

(101)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}[v,v]=

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}[v_{0},v_{0}]+\varepsilon\mathcal{B}[v_{0},v_{1}]+\varepsilon\mathcal{B}[v_{1},v_{0}]+\varepsilon^{2}\mathcal{B}[v_{1},v_{1}]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c(M)\|v_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}-C\varepsilon(\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}+\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c(M)\|v\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}-C(M)\varepsilon\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2},

where we have used Lemma
5.16
, inequality (
100
) and taken
\varepsilon
small enough.

Finally, from (
101
) and (
92
) we achieve the conclusion of the lemma. Recall that (
92
) tell us that

\displaystyle B[(u,v),(u,v)]

\displaystyle=2\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}+\varepsilon\mathcal{B}[v,v]-C\varepsilon\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}-C\varepsilon^{2}\|v\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}

\displaystyle\geq 2\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}+c(M)\varepsilon\|v\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}-C(M)\varepsilon^{2}\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}-C\varepsilon\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}-C\varepsilon^{2}\|v\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}

\displaystyle\geq c(M)\left(\|u\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|v\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}\right),

where in the last step we are taking
\varepsilon
small enough.
∎

Then we have all the ingredients to prove the main result of this section. We just need to consider the functional equation

(102)

\displaystyle B[(u_{1},v_{1}),(u,v)]=-\frac{1}{m}(W^{+}_{m},u)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}-\frac{1}{m}(W^{-}_{m},v)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}},\quad\text{for all $(u,v)\in(-a,b)^{\perp}$.}

Combining Lemma
5.18
and Lax-Milgram theorem, there exists
(u_{1},v_{1})\in(-a,b)^{\perp}
satisfying (
102
). This implies that there exist
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle(T^{+}_{m}[u_{1},v_{1}],T^{-}_{m}[u_{1},v_{1}])=-\frac{1}{m}(W^{+}_{m},W^{-}_{m})+\gamma(-a,b).

But then, taking the inner product on
L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}([-1,1])\times L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}([-1,1])
against
(-a,b)
, we get

-(T^{+}_{m}[u_{1},v_{1}],a)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+(T^{-}_{m}[u_{1},v_{1}],b)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}=-\frac{1}{m}(-(W^{+}_{m},a)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+(W^{-}_{m},b)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}})+\gamma(\|a\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}+\|b\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}).

Since
T_{m}^{+}[a,b]=0
and
T_{m}^{-}[a,b]=0
, we have that

\displaystyle-(T^{+}_{m}[u_{1},v_{1}],a)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+(T^{-}_{m}[u_{1},v_{1}],b)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}

\displaystyle=0,\qquad\text{by Lemma }\ref {adjointness},

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{m}(-(W^{+}_{m},a)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+(W^{-}_{m},b)_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}})

\displaystyle=0,\qquad\text{by Lemma }\ref {necesario},

which implies that
\gamma=0
. Therefore, there exist
(u_{1},v_{1})\in L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}([-1,1])\times L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}([-1,1])
satisfying

(103)

\displaystyle(T^{+}_{m}[u_{1},v_{1}],T^{-}_{m}[u_{1},v_{1}])=-\frac{1}{m}(W^{+}_{m},W^{-}_{m}).

Next we shall improve the regularity of
(u_{1},v_{1})
. By (
97
) and (
102
), we have that

\displaystyle\|u_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|v_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(M)B[(u_{1},v_{1}),(u_{1},v_{1})]=-\frac{C(M)}{m}\left((W^{+}_{m},u_{1})_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+(W^{-}_{m},v_{1})_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(M)\left(\|W^{+}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}\|u_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+\|W^{-}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}\|v_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(M)\left(\|W^{+}_{m}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|W^{-}_{m}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\|u_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|v_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}\right),

where in the last step we have used the generalized Young inequality. Since
\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}\in L^{\infty}([-1,1]),
we get the right-hand side of the following expression in the unweighted
L^{2}([-1,1])-
norm.

(104)

\displaystyle\|u_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|v_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}^{2}\leq C(M)\left(\|W^{+}_{m}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|W^{-}_{m}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right).

Let us look to (
90
). We can write this equation in the following way

(105)

\displaystyle v_{1}(z)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\varepsilon}{2m\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})v_{1}(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon m|z-\bar{z}|}\mbox{d}\bar{z}

\displaystyle+\frac{\varepsilon}{2m\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)}e^{-2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})u_{1}(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon m(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}-\frac{1}{m\Lambda^{-}_{m}(z)}W^{-}_{m}(z).

Recalling (
94
) we have

(106)

\|1/\Lambda_{m}^{-}\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}\leq\frac{C(M)}{\varepsilon},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|\varepsilon/\Lambda_{m}^{-}\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}\leq C(M).

Then, taking the
L^{2}([-1,1])
norm of (
105
) yields

\displaystyle\|v_{1}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(M)\left(\|v_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+\|u_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|W^{-}_{m}\|_{L^{2}}\right),

and using inequality (
104
) give us

\displaystyle\|v_{1}\|_{L^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C(M)}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\|W^{+}_{m}\|_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|W^{-}_{m}\|_{L^{2}}\right)+C(M)\left(\|W^{+}_{m}\|_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|W^{-}_{m}\|_{L^{2}}\right)+\frac{C(M)}{\varepsilon}\|W^{-}_{m}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C(M)}{\varepsilon}\left(\|W^{+}_{m}\|_{L^{2}}+\|W^{-}_{m}\|_{L^{2}}\right).

Once we have proved that
v_{1}\in L^{2}([-1,1])
we only have to take derivatives on expression (
105
), but before that, we observe that

(107)

\|1/\Lambda_{m}^{-}\|_{\dot{W}^{k,\infty}([-1,1])}\leq\frac{C(M)}{\varepsilon},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|\varepsilon/\Lambda_{m}^{-}\|_{\dot{W}^{k,\infty}([-1,1])}\leq C(M),\quad\text{for }k=1,2.

For
k=3
we need to distinguish between degenerate (
\kappa=0
) and non-degenerate (
\kappa>0
) case:

(108)

\|1/\Lambda_{m}^{-}\|_{\dot{W}^{3,\infty}([-1,1])}\leq\begin{cases}\frac{C(M)}{\varepsilon\kappa}\quad&\kappa>0,\\
\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\quad&\kappa=0,\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\varepsilon/\Lambda_{m}^{-}\|_{\dot{W}^{3,\infty}([-1,1])}\leq\begin{cases}\frac{C(M)}{\kappa}\quad&\kappa>0,\\
C\quad&\kappa=0.\end{cases}

Proof of (
107
).

Note that

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{1)}\left(\frac{1}{\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)}\right)

\displaystyle=-\partial_{z}\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)\left(\frac{1}{\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)}\right)^{2},

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{2)}\left(\frac{1}{\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)}\right)

\displaystyle=-\partial_{z}^{2)}\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)\left(\frac{1}{\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)}\right)^{2}-2\partial_{z}^{1)}\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)\left(\frac{1}{\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)}\right)\partial_{z}^{1)}\left(\frac{1}{\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)}\right),

with

\displaystyle\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)

\displaystyle=(1+\lambda_{1}-z)\varepsilon+(\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}+\Phi_{\kappa}(z))\varepsilon^{2},

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon+\Phi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z)\varepsilon^{2},

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{2}\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{\kappa}^{\prime\prime}(z)\varepsilon^{2},

give us (
107
) for
k=1
by (
106
). Similarly, we get (
107
) for
k=2
by (
106
) and (
107
) for
k=1.

∎

Proof of (
108
).

For
k=3
we have the expression

(109)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{3)}\left(\frac{1}{\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)}\right)=

\displaystyle-\partial_{z}^{3)}\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)\left(\frac{1}{\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)}\right)^{2}-4\partial_{z}^{2)}\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)\left(\frac{1}{\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)}\right)\partial_{z}^{1)}\left(\frac{1}{\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)}\right)

\displaystyle-2\partial_{z}^{1)}\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)\left(\partial_{z}^{1)}\left(\frac{1}{\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-2\partial_{z}^{1)}\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)\left(\frac{1}{\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)}\right)\partial_{z}^{2)}\left(\frac{1}{\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)}\right).

The difference between degenerate and non-degenerate case is due to the factor:

\partial_{z}^{3}\Lambda_{m}^{-}(z)=\Phi_{\kappa}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z)\varepsilon^{2}=\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime\prime}(z)\varepsilon^{2}.

Note that for the degenerate case, we have that
\varphi=\frac{1-z}{2}
is a linear function and the above term vanishes. In contrast, for the non-degenerate case (
\kappa>0
) we obtain the bound

\|\partial_{z}^{3}\Lambda_{m}^{-}\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}=\|\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}\varepsilon^{2}\leq\frac{C}{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2}.

Finally, by taking the
L^{\infty}([-1,1])-
norm on both sides of (
109
) and combining previous estimates (
106
) and (
107
) we arrive to the required result.
∎

Remark 5.19
.

Notice that the bound obtained in (
108
) is completely natural due to the fact that

\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z)\xrightarrow{\kappa\to 0}-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{[-1,1]}(z),\quad\text{in }H^{\frac{3}{2}-}(\mathbb{R}),

but not in higher-order regularity spaces. Then, for
\kappa>0
the Sobolev embedding just give us

\|\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}\lesssim\|\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}([-1,1])}\leq\frac{C}{\kappa}.

Therefore, we have proved

\displaystyle\|v_{1}\|_{H^{k}}\leq c_{\kappa}\frac{C(M)}{\varepsilon}\left(\|W^{+}_{m}\|_{H^{k}}+\|W^{-}_{m}\|_{H^{k}}\right),

for
k=0,1,2
and
3
with

(110)

c_{\kappa}:=\begin{cases}1\qquad&\text{if }\kappa=0,\\
\kappa^{-1}\qquad&\text{if }\kappa>0.\end{cases}

Proceeding similarly for
u_{1}
, we have the expression:

(111)

\displaystyle u_{1}(z)=

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{2m\Lambda_{m}^{+}(z)}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})u_{1}(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon m|z-\bar{z}|}\mbox{d}\bar{z}

\displaystyle-\frac{\varepsilon}{2m\Lambda_{m}^{+}(z)}e^{-2m}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})v_{1}(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon m(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}-\frac{1}{m\Lambda^{+}_{m}(z)}W^{+}_{m}(z).

Now, since

\displaystyle\Lambda_{m}^{+}(z)

\displaystyle=2+(-1+\lambda_{1}+z)\varepsilon+(\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}-\Phi_{\kappa}(z))\varepsilon^{2},

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Lambda_{m}^{+}(z)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon-\Phi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(z)\varepsilon^{2},

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{2}\Lambda_{m}^{+}(z)

\displaystyle=-\Phi_{\kappa}^{\prime\prime}(z)\varepsilon^{2},

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{3}\Lambda_{m}^{+}(z)

\displaystyle=-\Phi_{\kappa}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z)\varepsilon^{2},

we observe that

\|1/\Lambda_{m}^{+}\|_{\dot{W}^{k,\infty}([-1,1])}\leq\varepsilon^{k},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|\varepsilon/\Lambda_{m}^{+}\|_{\dot{W}^{k,\infty}([-1,1])}\leq\varepsilon^{k+1},\quad\text{for }k=0,1,2.

and

\|1/\Lambda_{m}^{-}\|_{\dot{W}^{3,\infty}([-1,1])}\leq c_{\kappa}\varepsilon^{2}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|\varepsilon/\Lambda_{m}^{-}\|_{\dot{W}^{3,\infty}([-1,1])}\leq c_{\kappa}\varepsilon^{3}.

Therefore, we have proved that

\displaystyle\|u_{1}\|_{H^{k}}\leq c_{\kappa}\left(\|W^{+}_{m}\|_{H^{k}}+\|W^{-}_{m}\|_{H^{k}}\right),

for
k=0,1,2,3
and
c_{\kappa}
given by (
110
).

5.4.2.
The case
n\neq m
.

In this case we have to solve

(112)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{+}_{m}(z)u(z)-\frac{\varepsilon}{2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})u(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon n|z-\bar{z}|}\mbox{d}\bar{z}+\frac{\varepsilon e^{-2n}}{2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})v(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon n(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}=-\frac{1}{n}W^{+}_{n}(z),

(113)

\displaystyle\frac{\Lambda^{-}_{m}(z)}{\varepsilon}v(z)+\frac{1}{2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})v(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon n|z-\bar{z}|}\mbox{d}\bar{z}-\frac{e^{-2n}}{2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})u(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon n(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}=-\frac{1}{n\varepsilon}W^{-}_{n}(z).

Since
\Lambda^{+}_{m}(z)=O(1)
we can divide (
112
) by
\Lambda^{+}_{m}(z)
to get

(114)

\displaystyle u(z)=-\frac{1}{n\Lambda^{+}_{m}(z)}W^{+}_{n}(z)+\varepsilon U[u,v](z),

where

\displaystyle U[u,v](z):=\frac{1}{2n\Lambda^{+}_{m}(z)}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})u(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon n|z-\bar{z}|}\mbox{d}\bar{z}-\frac{e^{-2n}}{2n\Lambda^{+}_{m}(z)}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})v(\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon n(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}.

And then we can write (
113
), using (
114
) as

(115)

\displaystyle(1+\lambda_{1}-z)v(z)+\frac{1}{2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})v(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n\varepsilon}W^{-}_{n}(z)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-2n}}{2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})\left(-\frac{1}{n\Lambda^{+}_{m}(\bar{z})}W^{+}_{n}(\bar{z})\right)e^{-\varepsilon n(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\varepsilon V[u,v](z),

where

\displaystyle V[u,v](z):=

\displaystyle-(\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}+\Phi_{\kappa}(z))v(z)+\frac{1}{2n}e^{-2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})U[u,v](\bar{z})e^{-\varepsilon n(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})v(\bar{z})\frac{e^{-n\varepsilon|z-\bar{z}|}-1}{\varepsilon}\mbox{d}\bar{z}.

In order to solve (
115
), we will use the following lemma.

Lemma 5.20
.

Let
F\in L^{2}([-1,1])
. Then, the unique solution to

(116)

\displaystyle(1+\lambda_{1}-z)f(z)+\frac{1}{2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})f(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}=F(z),

is given by

\displaystyle f(z)=\frac{1}{1+\lambda_{1}-z}\left(F(z)+\frac{1}{2(m-n)}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})\frac{F(\bar{z})}{1+\lambda_{1}-\bar{z}}\mbox{d}\bar{z}\right).

Proof.

If
f(z)
is a solution of (
116
) then

(117)

\displaystyle f(z)=\frac{C}{1+\lambda_{1}-z}+\frac{F(z)}{1+\lambda_{1}-z},

for some constant
C
. Plugging this expression of
f
into (
116
) yields

\displaystyle C+\frac{C}{2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})}{1+\lambda_{1}-\bar{z}}\mbox{d}\bar{z}+\frac{1}{2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})\frac{F(\bar{z})}{1+\lambda_{1}-\bar{z}}\mbox{d}\bar{z}=0,

Then, using (
63
) we get

\displaystyle C\left(1-\frac{2m}{2n}\right)=-\frac{1}{2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})\frac{F(\bar{z})}{1+\lambda_{1}-\bar{z}}\mbox{d}\bar{z},

or

(118)

\displaystyle C=\frac{1}{2(m-n)}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})\frac{F(\bar{z})}{1+\lambda_{1}-\bar{z}}\mbox{d}\bar{z}.

Conversely, it is easy to check that
f
in (
117
), with
C
given by (
118
), solves the equation (
116
).
∎

To finish, let us introduce

\displaystyle I[F](z):=\frac{1}{1+\lambda_{1}-z}\left(F(z)+\frac{1}{2(m-n)}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})\frac{F(\bar{z})}{1+\lambda_{1}-\bar{z}}\mbox{d}\bar{z}\right),

and

\displaystyle W(z):=-\frac{1}{n\varepsilon}W^{-}_{n}(z)+\frac{e^{-2n}}{2n}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})\left(-\frac{1}{n\Lambda^{+}_{m}(\bar{z})}W^{+}_{n}(\bar{z})\right)e^{-\varepsilon n(z+\bar{z})}\mbox{d}\bar{z}.

By using Lemma
5.20
, equation (
115
) can be written as

(119)

\displaystyle v(z)=I[W](z)+\varepsilon I[V[u,v]](z).

The coupled system given by (
114
) and (
119
) is a linear contraction on
L^{2}([-1,1])
for
\varepsilon
small enough. Therefore, there exists a unique
(u,v)\in L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])
solving (
114
), (
119
) with

\displaystyle\|u\|_{L^{2}}+\|v\|_{L^{2}}\leq\frac{C(M)}{\varepsilon n}\left(\|W^{+}_{n}\|_{L^{2}}+\|W^{-}_{n}\|_{L^{2}}\right)

Therefore, this
(u,v)
is the unique solution of (
112
) and (
113
). Taking derivatives on (
114
) and (
119
) we find that actually

\displaystyle\|u\|_{H^{k}}+\|v\|_{H^{k}}\leq c_{\kappa}\frac{C(M)}{\varepsilon n}\left(\|W^{+}_{n}\|_{H^{k}}+\|W^{-}_{n}\|_{H^{k}}\right)

for
k=0,1,2,3
and
c_{\kappa}
given by (
110
).

Then we have achieved all the conclusions of Theorem
5.15
.
∎

5.5.
The transversality property

To check the complete assumptions of Crandall-Rabinowitz’s theorem it remains to prove the transversality assumption. In order to do this is enough to show that

D_{\lambda,\mathsf{f}}^{2}F[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m},0]h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\not\in\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]),\qquad\text{where}\qquad\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}])=\text{span }\{h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\}.

This will be done in a straightforward way without any difficulty. Recall that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
we have proved before that there exist an eigenvalue
\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\in\mathbb{R}
given by Theorem
5.1
such that
\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}])=\text{span }\{h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\},
where
h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}({\bf x})=h(y)\cos(mx)
and with
h
given by

(120)

h(y)=\left\{\begin{split}a(\tfrac{+y-1}{\varepsilon})\qquad&\text{if}\quad y\in[+1-\varepsilon,+1+\varepsilon],\\
b(\tfrac{-y-1}{\varepsilon})\qquad&\text{if}\quad y\in[-1-\varepsilon,-1+\varepsilon].\end{split}\right.

To finish, let us proceed by reduction to absurd. Since
D_{\lambda,\mathsf{f}}^{2}F[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m},0]h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}=-h(y)m\sin(mx),
using Lemma
5.14
we have that if
D_{\lambda,\mathsf{f}}^{2}F[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m},0]h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\in\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{L}_{\kappa,\varepsilon}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]),
then

-\|a\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varphi_{\kappa}}}+\|b\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\varphi_{\kappa}}}=0.

This give us a contradiction since
\|a\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}=O(\varepsilon)
and
\|b\|_{L^{2}_{\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}=O(1)
and concludes the last required condition of Crandall-Rabinowitz theorem
3.1
.

6.
Main theorem

After verifying all the conditions for the application of the Crandall-Rabinowitz theorem
3.1
and the discussion in Section
2
we obtain the following theorem:

Theorem 6.1
.

Fixed
1<M<\infty
. There exist
\varepsilon_{0}(M)
,
\kappa_{0}(M)
such that, for every
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
,
0<\kappa<\kappa_{0}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
m<M
, there exist a branch of solutions,
\mathsf{f}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}^{\sigma}\in H^{4,3}(D_{\varepsilon})
parameterize by
\sigma
, of equation (
16
), with
|\sigma|<\sigma_{0}
, for some small number
\sigma_{0}>0
,
\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}
as in section
2.3
and
\lambda=\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}.
These solutions satisfy:

(1)

\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(x,y)
is
\frac{2\pi}{m}-
periodic on
x
.

(2)

The branch

\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}=\sigma h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}+o(\sigma)\quad\text{ in $H^{4,3}(D_{\varepsilon})$},

and the speed

\lambda^{\sigma}=\lambda_{\varepsilon,\,\kappa,m}+o(\sigma),

where
(\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,\sigma},\,h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m})
are given in Theorem
5.1
and Remark
5.4
.

(3)

\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(x,y)
depends on
x
in a nontrivial way.

In addition, the vorticity
\omega^{\sigma}_{\kappa,\sigma,m}\in H^{4,3}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R})
, given implicitly by

\displaystyle\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(x_{1},x_{2})=\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(y),

for
(x_{1},x_{2})=(x,y+f^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(x,y))
with
x\in\mathbb{T}
and
y\in[-1-\varepsilon,-1+\varepsilon]\cup[1-\varepsilon,1+\varepsilon]
,

\displaystyle\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(x_{1},x_{2})=\varepsilon,

for
x_{1}\in\mathbb{T}
and
-1+\varepsilon+f^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(x_{1},-1+\varepsilon)<x_{2}<1-\varepsilon+f^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(x_{1},1-\varepsilon)
, and

\displaystyle\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(x_{1},x_{2})=0,

for
x_{1}\in\mathbb{T}
and either
x_{2}>1+\varepsilon+f^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(x_{1},1+\varepsilon)
or
x_{2}<-1-\varepsilon+f^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(x_{1},-1-\varepsilon)
,

yields a traveling way solution for 2D Euler in the sense that

\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(x_{1}+\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}t,x_{2})

satisfies the system (
2
). Importantly,
\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(x_{1},x_{2})
depends non trivially on
x_{1}
.

Then, in order to prove Theorem
1.1
it remains to prove that
H^{\frac{3}{2}-}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R})
-norm of
\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
can be made as small as we want and that
\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,\sigma}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R})
. We do this in Theorems
6.2
and
6.4
.

6.1.
Distance of the traveling wave to the Couette flow

The solution
\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
obtained in Theorem
6.1
satisfies the following statement:

Theorem 6.2
.

Fixed
M>1
,
0<\kappa<\kappa_{0}
and
0<\gamma<\frac{3}{2}
, for all
\epsilon>0
and
1\leq m<M
, there exist
\varepsilon>0
and
\sigma>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\|_{H^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R})}<\epsilon.

Proof.

Let us emphasis that we can make
\|\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\|_{H^{4,3}}
arbitrarily small fixed
\varepsilon
,
\kappa
and
m
, taking
\sigma
small. We have all the ingredients to obtain a quantitative estimate of the distance between the Couette flow and the constructed traveling wave. We could compute the
H^{\frac{3}{2}-}
norm of
w^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
from the expression

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\gamma}w^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}({\bf x})=k_{\gamma}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{w^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}({\bf x})-w^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}({\bf y})}{|{\bf x}-{\bf y}|^{2+\gamma}}\mbox{d}{\bf y}.

We could show that this quantity is as small as we want by making
\sigma
and
\varepsilon
small with independence of
\kappa
. Remember that
\varpi_{\varepsilon,0}
is in
H^{\frac{3}{2}-}
. However, to avoid tedious computations, we will take a shortcut using interpolation of Sobolev norms:

\|g\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{2}}^{1-s}\|g\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}^{s},\qquad(0<s<1),

which (taking
0<\gamma\ll 1
) give us

(121)

\|w+1\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3-\gamma}{2}}}\equiv\|\omega\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3-\gamma}{2}}}\lesssim\|\omega\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\|\omega\|_{\dot{H}^{2}}^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}.

Remark 6.3
.

This way of proceeding will make us lose the independence on
\kappa
. But since we are actually interested on the case
\kappa>0
it will be good enough.

To alleviate the notation let us skip the subscripts
(\varepsilon,\kappa,m)
and the superscript
\sigma
on
w^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
,
\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
, and
\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
in the rest of the section. We will keep
\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}
and
\varphi_{\kappa}
as we did before.

In order to compute the right-hand side of (
121
), we have that

(122)

\nabla\omega(x,y+\mathsf{f}({\bf x}))=\frac{\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(y)}{1+\mathsf{f}_{y}({\bf x})}(-\mathsf{f}_{x}({\bf x}),1),\quad\text{on $\supp(\nabla\omega)$},

thus, making the appropriate change of variables, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\omega\|_{\dot{H}^{2}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla^{2}\omega({\bf x})\right|^{2}\mbox{d}{\bf x}=\int_{\supp(\nabla^{2}\omega)}\left|\partial^{2}\omega({\bf x})\right|^{2}\mbox{d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{D_{\varepsilon}}|\nabla^{2}\omega(x,y+\mathsf{f}({\bf x}))|^{2}(1+\mathsf{f}_{y}({\bf x}))\mbox{d}{\bf x},

and computing second order derivatives we get

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{2}\omega(x,y+\mathsf{f}({\bf x}))=

\displaystyle A_{1}({\bf x})\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(y)+A_{2}({\bf x})\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}(y),

\displaystyle\partial_{y}^{2}\omega(x,y+\mathsf{f}({\bf x}))=

\displaystyle A_{3}({\bf x})\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(y)+A_{4}({\bf x})\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}(y),

\displaystyle\partial_{xy}^{2}\omega(x,y+\mathsf{f}({\bf x}))=

\displaystyle A_{5}({\bf x})\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(y)+A_{6}({\bf x})\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}(y),

where
A_{i}
for
i=1,...,6
are functions which depend on
\partial_{x}\mathsf{f},\partial_{y}\mathsf{f},\partial^{2}_{x}\mathsf{f},\partial^{2}_{y}\mathsf{f},\partial^{2}_{xy}\mathsf{f}
.

The
H^{4,3}-
norm of
\mathsf{f}
could depend on
\varepsilon
badly. However we always could choose
\sigma
small enough in such a way that the
H^{4,3}
-norm of
\mathsf{f}
is small. Noticing that
H^{4,3}\subset C^{2}
(see
[
15
, Lemma 4.1]
) we have that
A_{i}
for
i=1,...,6
are bounded functions.
Then, we have that

\displaystyle\|\omega\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle\approx\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(I_{\varepsilon})},

\displaystyle\|\omega\|_{\dot{H}^{2}}

\displaystyle\approx\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(I_{\varepsilon})}+\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}(I_{\varepsilon})}.

Let us note that by parity of the profile
\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}
on
I_{\varepsilon}=[-1-\varepsilon,-1+\varepsilon]\cup[+1-\varepsilon,+1+\varepsilon]
, we can just reduced our problem to study the following norms:

\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}([1-\varepsilon,1+\varepsilon])},\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}([1-\varepsilon,1+\varepsilon])}.

Recalling the definitions of
\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}
and
\varphi_{\kappa}
in section
2.3
. We have that

\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(y+1)=-\frac{\psi^{\prime}_{\kappa}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)}{\int_{-1}^{1}\psi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}},\qquad\text{for }|y|\leq\varepsilon.

At this point it does not matter to consider the normalization factor
\int_{-1}^{1}\psi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z}
. Then

(123)

\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}([1-\varepsilon,1+\varepsilon])}=\int_{1-\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(y)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}y=\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}\left|\psi_{\kappa}^{\prime}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\right|^{2}\,\mbox{d}y\leq 2\varepsilon\|\psi_{\kappa}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}^{2},

and

(124)

\displaystyle\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}([1-\varepsilon,1+\varepsilon])}=\int_{1-\varepsilon}^{1+\varepsilon}|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}(y)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}y=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}\left|\psi_{\kappa}^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\right|^{2}\,\mbox{d}y.

Since

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z)=\int_{-1+\kappa}^{1+\kappa}\Theta_{\kappa}(z-\bar{z})\mbox{d}\bar{z},

thus

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\kappa}(z)=\Theta_{\kappa}(z-(1-\kappa))-\Theta_{\kappa}(z-(-1+\kappa)),

and

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime\prime}_{\kappa}\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)=\frac{1}{\kappa}\left(\Theta\left(\frac{y-\varepsilon(1-\kappa)}{\varepsilon\kappa}\right)-\Theta\left(\frac{y-\varepsilon(-1+\kappa)}{\varepsilon\kappa}\right)\right).

Putting the above expression into (
124
) we get

\displaystyle\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}([1-\varepsilon,1+\varepsilon])}\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}\kappa^{2}}\left(\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}\left|\Theta\left(\frac{y-\varepsilon(1-\kappa)}{\varepsilon\kappa}\right)\right|^{2}\,\mbox{d}y+\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\varepsilon}\left|\Theta\left(\frac{y-\varepsilon(-1+\kappa)}{\varepsilon\kappa}\right)\right|^{2}\,\mbox{d}y\right).

Now, we make the changes of variables
\tilde{y}=y-\varepsilon(1-\kappa)
in the first integral and
\tilde{y}=y-\varepsilon(-1+\kappa)
in the second one. Note that the limits of integration will be
(-\varepsilon(2-\kappa),\varepsilon\kappa)
and
(-\varepsilon\kappa,\varepsilon(2-\kappa))
, respectively. But
\varepsilon(2-\kappa)>\varepsilon\kappa
for
\kappa<1
, and the support of
\Theta(\tilde{y}/\varepsilon\kappa)
is inside of
(-\varepsilon\kappa,\varepsilon\kappa)
.
Thus both integrals run from
-\varepsilon\kappa
to
\varepsilon\kappa
:

(125)

\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}([1-\varepsilon,1+\varepsilon])}\leq\frac{2}{\varepsilon^{2}\kappa^{2}}\int_{-\varepsilon\kappa}^{\varepsilon\kappa}\left|\Theta\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon\kappa}\right)\right|^{2}\,\mbox{d}y\leq\frac{4}{\varepsilon\kappa}\|\Theta\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}^{2}.

Therefore, combining (
123
) and (
125
), there exists
C>0
such that

\|w+1\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3-\gamma}{2}}}\equiv\|\omega\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3-\gamma}{2}}}\lesssim\|\omega\|_{\dot{H}^{1}}^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\|\omega\|_{\dot{H}^{2}}^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{1+\gamma}{4}}\varepsilon^{\frac{-1+\gamma}{4}}\kappa^{\frac{-1+\gamma}{4}}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\kappa^{\frac{-1+\gamma}{4}}.

In addition

\displaystyle\|w+1\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\equiv\|\omega\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\varepsilon.

Consequently, for any
\epsilon>0
and for any
0<s<3/2
, taking
\varepsilon
and
\sigma
small enough, we find a traveling wave such that its vorticity satisfies

\|w^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}+1\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R})}<\epsilon.

∎

6.2.
Full regularity of the solution

This section is devote to proving the following result.

Theorem 6.4
.

The solution
\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
in Theorem
6.1
is actually
C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R})
.

Proof.

In order to prove this theorem we will use equation (
16
), i.e.,

\displaystyle(\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}+y+\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}({\bf x})-\mathfrak{u}_{1}[\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]({\bf x}))\partial_{x}\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}({\bf x})=\mathfrak{u}_{2}[\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]({\bf x}),\qquad{\bf x}\in D_{\varepsilon}=\mathbb{T}\times I_{\varepsilon},

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{u}_{1}[\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]({\bf x})=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}\times I_{\varepsilon}}\varpi^{\prime}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(\bar{y})\log\left(\cosh(y-\bar{y}+\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}({\bf x})-\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(\bar{{\bf x}}))-\cos(x-\bar{x})\right)\mbox{d}\bar{{\bf x}},

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{u}_{2}[\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]({\bf x})=

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}\times I_{\varepsilon}}\varpi^{\prime}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(\bar{y})\log\left(\cosh(y-\bar{y}+\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}({\bf x})-\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(\bar{{\bf x}}))-\cos(x-\bar{x})\right)\partial_{x}\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(\bar{{\bf x}})\mbox{d}\bar{{\bf x}}.

Let us remove the superscript
\sigma
and the subscripts
\varepsilon
,
\kappa
and
m
from
\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
to alleviate the notation.

First of all we notice that, since
\mathsf{f}\in X(D_{\varepsilon})
by construction, in particular it is mean zero in
x
. So, we can recover
\mathsf{f}
from
\partial_{x}\mathsf{f}
through the expression

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}({\bf x})=\text{Int}[\partial_{x}\mathsf{f}]({\bf x}):=\int_{0}^{x}\partial_{x}\mathsf{f}(\bar{x},y)\mbox{d}\bar{x}-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\left(\int_{0}^{\bar{x}}\partial_{x}\mathsf{f}(\tilde{x},y)\mbox{d}\tilde{x}\right)\mbox{d}\bar{x},\qquad x>0.

Therefore, if
\partial_{x}\mathsf{f}\in H^{k}(D_{\varepsilon})
then it is clear that
\mathsf{f}\in H^{k+1,k}(D_{\varepsilon})
. Next, we will show that if
\partial_{x}\mathsf{f}\in H^{k}(D_{\varepsilon})
, then in fact
\mathsf{f}\in H^{k+1}(D_{\varepsilon})
for
k\geq 3.
Using the above expressions we have that, if
\partial_{x}\mathsf{f}\in H^{k}(D_{\varepsilon})
then
\mathfrak{u}_{i}[\mathsf{f}]\in H^{k+1}(D_{\varepsilon})
for
i=1,2
. In addition, we can split
\mathfrak{u}_{1}[\mathsf{f}]
in the following way

\displaystyle\mathfrak{u}_{1}[\mathsf{f}]({\bf x})=\Omega_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(y)+\left(\mathfrak{u}_{1}[\mathsf{f}]({\bf x})-\Omega_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(y)\right),

where (see (
37
))

\displaystyle\Omega_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(y)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}\times I_{\varepsilon}}\varpi^{\prime}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(\bar{y})\log\left(\cosh(y-\bar{y})-\cos(x-\bar{x})\right)\mbox{d}\bar{{\bf x}}=\int_{0}^{y}\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(\bar{y})\mbox{d}\bar{y}.

Let us fix
k=3
, i.e.
\partial_{x}\mathsf{f}\in H^{3}(D_{\varepsilon})
and prove that in fact
\mathsf{f}\in H^{4}(D_{\varepsilon})
. Then we have

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{u}_{1}[\mathsf{f}]-\Omega_{\varepsilon,\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\sigma\,C_{\varepsilon,\kappa,M},

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{u}_{2}[\mathsf{f}]\|_{H^{4}}\leq\sigma\,C_{\varepsilon,\kappa,M},

and

\displaystyle\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}+y-\Omega_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(y)\geq\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\geq c_{\varepsilon,\kappa,M}>0.

Then, taking
\sigma
small enough we obtain

(126)

\displaystyle\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}+y+\mathsf{f}({\bf x})-\mathfrak{u}_{1}[\mathsf{f}]({\bf x})>c_{\varepsilon,\kappa,M},

and we can write

(127)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\mathsf{f}({\bf x})=\frac{1}{\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}+y+\mathsf{f}({\bf x})-\mathfrak{u}_{1}[\mathsf{f}]({\bf x})}\mathfrak{u}_{2}[\mathsf{f}]({\bf x}).

To get extra regularity on the vertical variable let us take three derivatives on
y
in (
127
) to obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\partial_{y}^{3}\mathsf{f}({\bf x})+\frac{\mathfrak{u}_{2}[f]({\bf x})}{\left(\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}+y+\mathsf{f}({\bf x})-\mathfrak{u}_{1}[\mathsf{f}]({\bf x})\right)^{2}}\partial^{3}_{y}\mathsf{f}({\bf x})=\text{terms at least in $H^{0,1}(D_{\varepsilon})$}.

For
\sigma
small enough such that (
126
) holds, let us define, for
g\in\{h\in L^{2}(D_{\varepsilon}):\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}h(x,y)\,\mbox{d}x=0\}
the operator

\displaystyle T[\mathsf{f}]g:=g+\frac{\mathfrak{u}_{2}[\mathsf{f}]}{\left(\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}+y+\mathsf{f}-\mathfrak{u}_{1}[\mathsf{f}]\right)^{2}}\text{Int}[g].

For any
F\in L^{2}(D_{\varepsilon})
there exists
T^{-1}[\mathsf{f}]
, such that, if
g\in\{h\in L^{2}(D_{\varepsilon}):\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}h(x,y)\,\mbox{d}x=0\}
satisfies

\displaystyle T[\mathsf{f}]g=F,

then

\displaystyle g=T^{-1}[\mathsf{f}]F.

In addition

T^{-1}[\mathsf{f}]:H^{0,1}(D_{\varepsilon})\to H^{0,1}(D_{\varepsilon}).

Therefore,
\partial_{x}\partial^{3}_{y}\mathsf{f}
is in
H^{0,1}(D_{\varepsilon})
. Finally, we can iterate this process to show that
\mathsf{f}
is in
C^{\infty}(D_{\varepsilon})
.

∎

7.
Appendix

In order to facilitate the presentation of the manuscript, we collect in this section all the technical lemmas used previously.
We start recalling the definition of the kernel given by

(128)

K[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}}):=\log\left[\cosh(\bar{y}+\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[g]({\bf x}))-\cos(\bar{x})\right],

where

\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[g]({\bf x}):=g({\bf x})-g({\bf x}-\bar{{\bf x}}).

In order to take derivatives into the kernel (
128
) we have introduced the following two functions

\displaystyle\Psi_{1}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})

\displaystyle:=\frac{\sinh(\bar{y}+\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[g]({\bf x}))}{\cosh(\bar{y}+\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[g]({\bf x}))-\cos(\bar{x})},

\displaystyle\Psi_{2}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})

\displaystyle:=\frac{\cosh(\bar{y}+\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[g]({\bf x}))}{\cosh(\bar{y}+\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[g]({\bf x}))-\cos(\bar{x})},

with derivatives given by the expressions

(129)

\displaystyle\partial_{{\bf x}}\Psi_{1}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})

\displaystyle=[\Psi_{2}[g]-\Psi_{1}^{2}[g]]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[\partial_{{\bf x}}g]({\bf x}),

(130)

\displaystyle\partial_{{\bf x}}\Psi_{2}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})

\displaystyle=[\Psi_{1}[g]\left(1-\Psi_{2}[g]\right)]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[\partial_{{\bf x}}g]({\bf x}).

Combining (
128
) with (
129
) and (
130
) it is just a matter of algebra to obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{{\bf x}}K[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})

\displaystyle=\Psi_{1}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[\partial_{{\bf x}}g]({\bf x}),

\displaystyle\partial_{{\bf x}}^{2}K[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})

\displaystyle=[\Psi_{2}[g]-\Psi_{1}^{2}[g]]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})\left(\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[\partial_{{\bf x}}g]({\bf x})\right)^{2}+\Psi_{1}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[\partial_{{\bf x}}^{2}g]({\bf x}),

\displaystyle\partial_{{\bf x}}^{3}K[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})

\displaystyle=\Psi_{1}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})[1-3\Psi_{2}[g]+2\Psi_{1}^{2}[g]]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})\left(\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[\partial_{{\bf x}}g]({\bf x})\right)^{3}

\displaystyle\quad+3[\Psi_{2}[g]-\Psi_{1}^{2}[g]]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[\partial_{{\bf x}}g]({\bf x})\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[\partial_{{\bf x}}^{2}g]({\bf x})+\Psi_{1}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[\partial_{{\bf x}}^{3}g]({\bf x}).

Now, before starting with the proof of the lemmas, we remember the Taylor expansion of the trigonometric functions involved in the definition of
\Psi_{i}({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})
for
i=1,2.

\sinh(z):=\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{z^{2n+1}}{(2n+1)!},\qquad\cosh(z):=\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{z^{2n}}{(2n)!},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\cos(z):=\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}z^{2n}}{(2n)!}.

Using the above expressions, it is simple to check that there exists some constant
C(\|g\|_{H^{3}(D_{\varepsilon})})
such that for any
\bar{{\bf x}}\in\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
with
|\bar{{\bf x}}|\ll 1
, we get

\displaystyle|\cosh(\bar{y}+\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[g]({\bf x}))|

\displaystyle\leq C(\|g\|_{H^{3}(D_{\varepsilon})}),

\displaystyle|\sinh(\bar{y}+\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[g]({\bf x}))|

\displaystyle\leq C(\|g\|_{H^{3}(D_{\varepsilon})})|\bar{{\bf x}}|,

(131)

\displaystyle|\cosh(\bar{y}+\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[g]({\bf x}))-\cos(\bar{x})|

\displaystyle\geq\left(\tfrac{1}{2}-\|g\|_{H^{3}(D_{\varepsilon})}\right)|\bar{{\bf x}}|^{2}-C(\|g\|_{H^{3}(D_{\varepsilon})})|\bar{{\bf x}}|^{4},

and as an immediate consequence we obtain the following result.

Corollary 7.1
.

Let
g\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}(H^{3}(D_{\varepsilon}))
with
0<\delta(\varepsilon)\ll 1
. For
\bar{{\bf x}}\in\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
such that
|\bar{{\bf x}}|\ll 1
we have

|\Psi_{i}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})|\leq\frac{C(\|g\|_{H^{3}(D_{\varepsilon})})}{1-C(\|g\|_{H^{3}(D_{\varepsilon})})}\frac{1}{|\bar{{\bf x}}|^{i}}\qquad\text{for}\quad i=1,2.

Now, we focus our attention in the higher order derivatives
\partial_{{\bf x}}^{2}K[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})
and
\partial_{{\bf x}}^{3}K[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})
. It will be convenient to introduce some notation in order to handle these terms. To be more specific, we will use the following expressions:

(132)

\displaystyle\partial_{{\bf x}}^{2}K[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{I}_{1}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})+\mathsf{I}_{2}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}}),

(133)

\displaystyle\partial_{{\bf x}}^{3}K[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{J}_{1}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})+\mathsf{J}_{2}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})+\mathsf{J}_{3}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}}),

where

\begin{split}\mathsf{I}_{1}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})&:=\tilde{\mathsf{I}}_{1}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})\left(\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[\partial_{{\bf x}}g]({\bf x})\right)^{2},\\
\mathsf{I}_{2}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})&:=\tilde{\mathsf{I}}_{2}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[\partial_{{\bf x}}^{2}g]({\bf x}),\\
\mathsf{J}_{1}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})&:=\tilde{\mathsf{J}}_{1}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})\left(\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[\partial_{{\bf x}}g]({\bf x})\right)^{3},\\
\mathsf{J}_{2}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})&:=\tilde{\mathsf{J}}_{2}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[\partial_{{\bf x}}g]({\bf x})\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[\partial_{{\bf x}}^{2}g]({\bf x}),\\
\mathsf{J}_{3}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})&:=\tilde{\mathsf{J}}_{3}[g]({\bf x},\bar{{\bf x}})\Delta_{\bar{{\bf x}}}[\partial_{{\bf x}}^{3}g]({\bf x}),\end{split}\quad\quad\begin{split}\tilde{\mathsf{I}}_{1}[g]&:=\Psi_{2}[g]-\Psi_{1}^{2}[g],\\
\tilde{\mathsf{I}}_{2}[g]&:=\Psi_{1}[g],\\
\tilde{\mathsf{J}}_{1}[g]&:=\Psi_{1}[g]\left(1-3\Psi_{2}[g]+2\Psi_{1}^{2}[g]\right),\\
\tilde{\mathsf{J}}_{2}[g]&:=3\Psi_{2}[g]-\Psi_{1}^{2}[g],\\
\tilde{\mathsf{J}}_{3}[g]&:=\Psi_{1}[g].\end{split}

As an immediate consequence of Corollary
7.1
we get the following bounds for the above expressions.

Corollary 7.2
.

Let
g\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}(H^{3}(D_{\varepsilon}))
with
0<\delta(\varepsilon)\ll 1
. For
\bar{{\bf x}}\in\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
